marked\n- [ ] Examples provided\n- [ ] Schema introspection enabled\n", "sector": "procedural", "salience": 1.0}, {"id": "skill_45", "label": "references", "group": "industrial", "content": "# GraphQL Schema Design Patterns\n\n## Schema Organization\n\n### Modular Schema Structure\n\n```graphql\n# user.graphql\ntype User {\n  id: ID!\n  email: String!\n  name: String!\n  posts: [Post!]!\n}\n\nextend type Query {\n  user(id: ID!): User\n  users(first: Int, after: String): UserConnection!\n}\n\nextend type Mutation {\n  createUser(input: CreateUserInput!): CreateUserPayload!\n}\n\n# post.graphql\ntype Post {\n  id: ID!\n  title: String!\n  content: String!\n  author: User!\n}\n\nextend type Query {\n  post(id: ID!): Post\n}\n```\n\n## Type Design Patterns\n\n### 1. Non-Null Types\n\n```graphql\ntype User {\n  id: ID! # Always required\n  email: String! # Required\n  phone: String # Optional (nullable)\n  posts: [Post!]! # Non-null array of non-null posts\n  tags: [String!] # Nullable array of non-null strings\n}\n```\n\n### 2. Interfaces for Polymorphism\n\n```graphql\ninterface Node {\n  id: ID!\n  createdAt: DateTime!\n}\n\ntype User implements Node {\n  id: ID!\n  createdAt: DateTime!\n  email: String!\n}\n\ntype Post implements Node {\n  id: ID!\n  createdAt: DateTime!\n  title: String!\n}\n\ntype Query {\n  node(id: ID!): Node\n}\n```\n\n### 3. Unions for Heterogeneous Results\n\n```graphql\nunion SearchResult = User | Post | Comment\n\ntype Query {\n  search(query: String!): [SearchResult!]!\n}\n\n# Query example\n{\n  search(query: \"graphql\") {\n    ... on User {\n      name\n      email\n    }\n    ... on Post {\n      title\n      content\n    }\n    ... on Comment {\n      text\n      author {\n        name\n      }\n    }\n  }\n}\n```\n\n### 4. Input Types\n\n```graphql\ninput CreateUserInput {\n  email: String!\n  name: String!\n  password: String!\n  profileInput: ProfileInput\n}\n\ninput ProfileInput {\n  bio: String\n  avatar: String\n  website: String\n}\n\ninput UpdateUserInput {\n  id: ID!\n  email: String\n  name: String\n  profileInput: ProfileInput\n}\n```\n\n## Pagination Patterns\n\n### Relay Cursor Pagination (Recommended)\n\n```graphql\ntype UserConnection {\n  edges: [UserEdge!]!\n  pageInfo: PageInfo!\n  totalCount: Int!\n}\n\ntype UserEdge {\n  node: User!\n  cursor: String!\n}\n\ntype PageInfo {\n  hasNextPage: Boolean!\n  hasPreviousPage: Boolean!\n  startCursor: String\n  endCursor: String\n}\n\ntype Query {\n  users(first: Int, after: String, last: Int, before: String): UserConnection!\n}\n\n# Usage\n{\n  users(first: 10, after: \"cursor123\") {\n    edges {\n      cursor\n      node {\n        id\n        name\n      }\n    }\n    pageInfo {\n      hasNextPage\n      endCursor\n    }\n  }\n}\n```\n\n### Offset Pagination (Simpler)\n\n```graphql\ntype UserList {\n  items: [User!]!\n  total: Int!\n  page: Int!\n  pageSize: Int!\n}\n\ntype Query {\n  users(page: Int = 1, pageSize: Int = 20): UserList!\n}\n```\n\n## Mutation Design Patterns\n\n### 1. Input/Payload Pattern\n\n```graphql\ninput CreatePostInput {\n  title: String!\n  content: String!\n  tags: [String!]\n}\n\ntype CreatePostPayload {\n  post: Post\n  errors: [Error!]\n  success: Boolean!\n}\n\ntype Error {\n  field: String\n  message: String!\n  code: String!\n}\n\ntype Mutation {\n  createPost(input: CreatePostInput!): CreatePostPayload!\n}\n```\n\n### 2. Optimistic Response Support\n\n```graphql\ntype UpdateUserPayload {\n  user: User\n  clientMutationId: String\n  errors: [Error!]\n}\n\ninput UpdateUserInput {\n  id: ID!\n  name: String\n  clientMutationId: String\n}\n\ntype Mutation {\n  updateUser(input: UpdateUserInput!): UpdateUserPayload!\n}\n```\n\n### 3. Batch Mutations\n\n```graphql\ninput BatchCreateUserInput {\n  users: [CreateUserInput!]!\n}\n\ntype BatchCreateUserPayload {\n  results: [CreateUserResult!]!\n  successCount: Int!\n  errorCount: Int!\n}\n\ntype CreateUserResult {\n  user: User\n  errors: [Error!]\n  index: Int!\n}\n\ntype Mutation {\n  batchCreateUsers(input: BatchCreateUserInput!): BatchCreateUserPayload!\n}\n```\n\n## Field Design\n\n### Arguments and Filtering\n\n```graphql\ntype Query {\n  posts(\n    # Pagination\n    first: Int = 20\n    after: String\n\n    # Filtering\n    status: PostStatus\n    authorId: ID\n    tag: String\n\n    # Sorting\n    orderBy: PostOrderBy = CREATED_AT\n    orderDirection: OrderDirection = DESC\n\n    # Searching\n    search: String\n  ): PostConnection!\n}\n\nenum PostStatus {\n  DRAFT\n  PUBLISHED\n  ARCHIVED\n}\n\nenum PostOrderBy {\n  CREATED_AT\n  UPDATED_AT\n  TITLE\n}\n\nenum OrderDirection {\n  ASC\n  DESC\n}\n```\n\n### Computed Fields\n\n```graphql\ntype User {\n  firstName: String!\n  lastName: String!\n  fullName: String! # Computed in resolver\n  posts: [Post!]!\n  postCount: Int! # Computed, doesn't load all posts\n}\n\ntype Post {\n  likeCount: Int!\n  commentCount: Int!\n  isLikedByViewer: Boolean! # Context-dependent\n}\n```\n\n## Subscriptions\n\n```graphql\ntype Subscription {\n  postAdded: Post!\n\n  postUpdated(postId: ID!): Post!\n\n  userStatusChanged(userId: ID!): UserStatus!\n}\n\ntype UserStatus {\n  userId: ID!\n  online: Boolean!\n  lastSeen: DateTime!\n}\n\n# Client usage\nsubscription {\n  postAdded {\n    id\n    title\n    author {\n      name\n    }\n  }\n}\n```\n\n## Custom Scalars\n\n```graphql\nscalar DateTime\nscalar Email\nscalar URL\nscalar JSON\nscalar Money\n\ntype User {\n  email: Email!\n  website: URL\n  createdAt: DateTime!\n  metadata: JSON\n}\n\ntype Product {\n  price: Money!\n}\n```\n\n## Directives\n\n### Built-in Directives\n\n```graphql\ntype User {\n  name: String!\n  email: String! @deprecated(reason: \"Use emails field instead\")\n  emails: [String!]!\n\n  # Conditional inclusion\n  privateData: PrivateData @include(if: $isOwner)\n}\n\n# Query\nquery GetUser($isOwner: Boolean!) {\n  user(id: \"123\") {\n    name\n    privateData @include(if: $isOwner) {\n      ssn\n    }\n  }\n}\n```\n\n### Custom Directives\n\n```graphql\ndirective @auth(requires: Role = USER) on FIELD_DEFINITION\n\nenum Role {\n  USER\n  ADMIN\n  MODERATOR\n}\n\ntype Mutation {\n  deleteUser(id: ID!): Boolean! @auth(requires: ADMIN)\n  updateProfile(input: ProfileInput!): User! @auth\n}\n```\n\n## Error Handling\n\n### Union Error Pattern\n\n```graphql\ntype User {\n  id: ID!\n  email: String!\n}\n\ntype ValidationError {\n  field: String!\n  message: String!\n}\n\ntype NotFoundError {\n  message: String!\n  resourceType: String!\n  resourceId: ID!\n}\n\ntype AuthorizationError {\n  message: String!\n}\n\nunion UserResult = User | ValidationError | NotFoundError | AuthorizationError\n\ntype Query {\n  user(id: ID!): UserResult!\n}\n\n# Usage\n{\n  user(id: \"123\") {\n    ... on User {\n      id\n      email\n    }\n    ... on NotFoundError {\n      message\n      resourceType\n    }\n    ... on AuthorizationError {\n      message\n    }\n  }\n}\n```\n\n### Errors in Payload\n\n```graphql\ntype CreateUserPayload {\n  user: User\n  errors: [Error!]\n  success: Boolean!\n}\n\ntype Error {\n  field: String\n  message: String!\n  code: ErrorCode!\n}\n\nenum ErrorCode {\n  VALIDATION_ERROR\n  UNAUTHORIZED\n  NOT_FOUND\n  INTERNAL_ERROR\n}\n```\n\n## N+1 Query Problem Solutions\n\n### DataLoader Pattern\n\n```python\nfrom aiodataloader import DataLoader\n\nclass PostLoader(DataLoader):\n    async def batch_load_fn(self, post_ids):\n        posts = await db.posts.find({\"id\": {\"$in\": post_ids}})\n        post_map = {post[\"id\"]: post for post in posts}\n        return [post_map.get(pid) for pid in post_ids]\n\n# Resolver\n@user_type.field(\"posts\")\nasync def resolve_posts(user, info):\n    loader = info.context[\"loaders\"][\"post\"]\n    return await loader.load_many(user[\"post_ids\"])\n```\n\n### Query Depth Limiting\n\n```python\nfrom graphql import GraphQLError\n\ndef depth_limit_validator(max_depth: int):\n    def validate(context, node, ancestors):\n        depth = len(ancestors)\n        if depth > max_depth:\n            raise GraphQLError(\n                f\"Query depth {depth} exceeds maximum {max_depth}\"\n            )\n    return validate\n```\n\n### Query Complexity Analysis\n\n```python\ndef complexity_limit_validator(max_complexity: int):\n    def calculate_complexity(node):\n        # Each field = 1, lists multiply\n        complexity = 1\n        if is_list_field(node):\n            complexity *= get_list_size_arg(node)\n        return complexity\n\n    return validate_complexity\n```\n\n## Schema Versioning\n\n### Field Deprecation\n\n```graphql\ntype User {\n  name: String! @deprecated(reason: \"Use firstName and lastName\")\n  firstName: String!\n  lastName: String!\n}\n```\n\n### Schema Evolution\n\n```graphql\n# v1 - Initial\ntype User {\n  name: String!\n}\n\n# v2 - Add optional field (backward compatible)\ntype User {\n  name: String!\n  email: String\n}\n\n# v3 - Deprecate and add new field\ntype User {\n  name: String! @deprecated(reason: \"Use firstName/lastName\")\n  firstName: String!\n  lastName: String!\n  email: String\n}\n```\n\n## Best Practices Summary\n\n1. **Nullable vs Non-Null**: Start nullable, make non-null when guaranteed\n2. **Input Types**: Always use input types for mutations\n3. **Payload Pattern**: Return errors in mutation payloads\n4. **Pagination**: Use cursor-based for infinite scroll, offset for simple cases\n5. **Naming**: Use camelCase for fields, PascalCase for types\n6. **Deprecation**: Use `@deprecated` instead of removing fields\n7. **DataLoaders**: Always use for relationships to prevent N+1\n8. **Complexity Limits**: Protect against expensive queries\n9. **Custom Scalars**: Use for domain-specific types (Email, DateTime)\n10. **Documentation**: Document all fields with descriptions\n", "sector": "procedural", "salience": 1.0}, {"id": "skill_46", "label": "references", "group": "industrial", "content": "# REST API Best Practices\n\n## URL Structure\n\n### Resource Naming\n\n```\n# Good - Plural nouns\nGET /api/users\nGET /api/orders\nGET /api/products\n\n# Bad - Verbs or mixed conventions\nGET /api/getUser\nGET /api/user  (inconsistent singular)\nPOST /api/createOrder\n```\n\n### Nested Resources\n\n```\n# Shallow nesting (preferred)\nGET /api/users/{id}/orders\nGET /api/orders/{id}\n\n# Deep nesting (avoid)\nGET /api/users/{id}/orders/{orderId}/items/{itemId}/reviews\n# Better:\nGET /api/order-items/{id}/reviews\n```\n\n## HTTP Methods and Status Codes\n\n### GET - Retrieve Resources\n\n```\nGET /api/users              ÔåÆ 200 OK (with list)\nGET /api/users/{id}         ÔåÆ 200 OK or 404 Not Found\nGET /api/users?page=2       ÔåÆ 200 OK (paginated)\n```\n\n### POST - Create Resources\n\n```\nPOST /api/users\n  Body: {\"name\": \"John\", \"email\": \"john@example.com\"}\n  ÔåÆ 201 Created\n  Location: /api/users/123\n  Body: {\"id\": \"123\", \"name\": \"John\", ...}\n\nPOST /api/users (validation error)\n  ÔåÆ 422 Unprocessable Entity\n  Body: {\"errors\": [...]}\n```\n\n### PUT - Replace Resources\n\n```\nPUT /api/users/{id}\n  Body: {complete user object}\n  ÔåÆ 200 OK (updated)\n  ÔåÆ 404 Not Found (doesn't exist)\n\n# Must include ALL fields\n```\n\n### PATCH - Partial Update\n\n```\nPATCH /api/users/{id}\n  Body: {\"name\": \"Jane\"}  (only changed fields)\n  ÔåÆ 200 OK\n  ÔåÆ 404 Not Found\n```\n\n### DELETE - Remove Resources\n\n```\nDELETE /api/users/{id}\n  ÔåÆ 204 No Content (deleted)\n  ÔåÆ 404 Not Found\n  ÔåÆ 409 Conflict (can't delete due to references)\n```\n\n## Filtering, Sorting, and Searching\n\n### Query Parameters\n\n```\n# Filtering\nGET /api/users?status=active\nGET /api/users?role=admin&status=active\n\n# Sorting\nGET /api/users?sort=created_at\nGET /api/users?sort=-created_at  (descending)\nGET /api/users?sort=name,created_at\n\n# Searching\nGET /api/users?search=john\nGET /api/users?q=john\n\n# Field selection (sparse fieldsets)\nGET /api/users?fields=id,name,email\n```\n\n## Pagination Patterns\n\n### Offset-Based Pagination\n\n```python\nGET /api/users?page=2&page_size=20\n\nResponse:\n{\n  \"items\": [...],\n  \"page\": 2,\n  \"page_size\": 20,\n  \"total\": 150,\n  \"pages\": 8\n}\n```\n\n### Cursor-Based Pagination (for large datasets)\n\n```python\nGET /api/users?limit=20&cursor=eyJpZCI6MTIzfQ\n\nResponse:\n{\n  \"items\": [...],\n  \"next_cursor\": \"eyJpZCI6MTQzfQ\",\n  \"has_more\": true\n}\n```\n\n### Link Header Pagination (RESTful)\n\n```\nGET /api/users?page=2\n\nResponse Headers:\nLink: <https://api.example.com/users?page=3>; rel=\"next\",\n      <https://api.example.com/users?page=1>; rel=\"prev\",\n      <https://api.example.com/users?page=1>; rel=\"first\",\n      <https://api.example.com/users?page=8>; rel=\"last\"\n```\n\n## Versioning Strategies\n\n### URL Versioning (Recommended)\n\n```\n/api/v1/users\n/api/v2/users\n\nPros: Clear, easy to route\nCons: Multiple URLs for same resource\n```\n\n### Header Versioning\n\n```\nGET /api/users\nAccept: application/vnd.api+json; version=2\n\nPros: Clean URLs\nCons: Less visible, harder to test\n```\n\n### Query Parameter\n\n```\nGET /api/users?version=2\n\nPros: Easy to test\nCons: Optional parameter can be forgotten\n```\n\n## Rate Limiting\n\n### Headers\n\n```\nX-RateLimit-Limit: 1000\nX-RateLimit-Remaining: 742\nX-RateLimit-Reset: 1640000000\n\nResponse when limited:\n429 Too Many Requests\nRetry-After: 3600\n```\n\n### Implementation Pattern\n\n```python\nfrom fastapi import HTTPException, Request\nfrom datetime import datetime, timedelta\n\nclass RateLimiter:\n    def __init__(self, calls: int, period: int):\n        self.calls = calls\n        self.period = period\n        self.cache = {}\n\n    def check(self, key: str) -> bool:\n        now = datetime.now()\n        if key not in self.cache:\n            self.cache[key] = []\n\n        # Remove old requests\n        self.cache[key] = [\n            ts for ts in self.cache[key]\n            if now - ts < timedelta(seconds=self.period)\n        ]\n\n        if len(self.cache[key]) >= self.calls:\n            return False\n\n        self.cache[key].append(now)\n        return True\n\nlimiter = RateLimiter(calls=100, period=60)\n\n@app.get(\"/api/users\")\nasync def get_users(request: Request):\n    if not limiter.check(request.client.host):\n        raise HTTPException(\n            status_code=429,\n            headers={\"Retry-After\": \"60\"}\n        )\n    return {\"users\": [...]}\n```\n\n## Authentication and Authorization\n\n### Bearer Token\n\n```\nAuthorization: Bearer eyJhbGciOiJIUzI1NiIs...\n\n401 Unauthorized - Missing/invalid token\n403 Forbidden - Valid token, insufficient permissions\n```\n\n### API Keys\n\n```\nX-API-Key: your-api-key-here\n```\n\n## Error Response Format\n\n### Consistent Structure\n\n```json\n{\n  \"error\": {\n    \"code\": \"VALIDATION_ERROR\",\n    \"message\": \"Request validation failed\",\n    \"details\": [\n      {\n        \"field\": \"email\",\n        \"message\": \"Invalid email format\",\n        \"value\": \"not-an-email\"\n      }\n    ],\n    \"timestamp\": \"2025-10-16T12:00:00Z\",\n    \"path\": \"/api/users\"\n  }\n}\n```\n\n### Status Code Guidelines\n\n- `200 OK`: Successful GET, PATCH, PUT\n- `201 Created`: Successful POST\n- `204 No Content`: Successful DELETE\n- `400 Bad Request`: Malformed request\n- `401 Unauthorized`: Authentication required\n- `403 Forbidden`: Authenticated but not authorized\n- `404 Not Found`: Resource doesn't exist\n- `409 Conflict`: State conflict (duplicate email, etc.)\n- `422 Unprocessable Entity`: Validation errors\n- `429 Too Many Requests`: Rate limited\n- `500 Internal Server Error`: Server error\n- `503 Service Unavailable`: Temporary downtime\n\n## Caching\n\n### Cache Headers\n\n```\n# Client caching\nCache-Control: public, max-age=3600\n\n# No caching\nCache-Control: no-cache, no-store, must-revalidate\n\n# Conditional requests\nETag: \"33a64df551425fcc55e4d42a148795d9f25f89d4\"\nIf-None-Match: \"33a64df551425fcc55e4d42a148795d9f25f89d4\"\nÔåÆ 304 Not Modified\n```\n\n## Bulk Operations\n\n### Batch Endpoints\n\n```python\nPOST /api/users/batch\n{\n  \"items\": [\n    {\"name\": \"User1\", \"email\": \"user1@example.com\"},\n    {\"name\": \"User2\", \"email\": \"user2@example.com\"}\n  ]\n}\n\nResponse:\n{\n  \"results\": [\n    {\"id\": \"1\", \"status\": \"created\"},\n    {\"id\": null, \"status\": \"failed\", \"error\": \"Email already exists\"}\n  ]\n}\n```\n\n## Idempotency\n\n### Idempotency Keys\n\n```\nPOST /api/orders\nIdempotency-Key: unique-key-123\n\nIf duplicate request:\nÔåÆ 200 OK (return cached response)\n```\n\n## CORS Configuration\n\n```python\nfrom fastapi.middleware.cors import CORSMiddleware\n\napp.add_middleware(\n    CORSMiddleware,\n    allow_origins=[\"https://example.com\"],\n    allow_credentials=True,\n    allow_methods=[\"*\"],\n    allow_headers=[\"*\"],\n)\n```\n\n## Documentation with OpenAPI\n\n```python\nfrom fastapi import FastAPI\n\napp = FastAPI(\n    title=\"My API\",\n    description=\"API for managing users\",\n    version=\"1.0.0\",\n    docs_url=\"/docs\",\n    redoc_url=\"/redoc\"\n)\n\n@app.get(\n    \"/api/users/{user_id}\",\n    summary=\"Get user by ID\",\n    response_description=\"User details\",\n    tags=[\"Users\"]\n)\nasync def get_user(\n    user_id: str = Path(..., description=\"The user ID\")\n):\n    \"\"\"\n    Retrieve user by ID.\n\n    Returns full user profile including:\n    - Basic information\n    - Contact details\n    - Account status\n    \"\"\"\n    pass\n```\n\n## Health and Monitoring Endpoints\n\n```python\n@app.get(\"/health\")\nasync def health_check():\n    return {\n        \"status\": \"healthy\",\n        \"version\": \"1.0.0\",\n        \"timestamp\": datetime.now().isoformat()\n    }\n\n@app.get(\"/health/detailed\")\nasync def detailed_health():\n    return {\n        \"status\": \"healthy\",\n        \"checks\": {\n            \"database\": await check_database(),\n            \"redis\": await check_redis(),\n            \"external_api\": await check_external_api()\n        }\n    }\n```\n", "sector": "procedural", "salience": 1.0}, {"id": "skill_47", "label": "sub-skills", "group": "industrial", "content": "# API Design Principles Implementation Playbook\n\nThis file contains detailed patterns, checklists, and code samples referenced by the skill.\n\n## Core Concepts\n\n### 1. RESTful Design Principles\n\n**Resource-Oriented Architecture**\n\n- Resources are nouns (users, orders, products), not verbs\n- Use HTTP methods for actions (GET, POST, PUT, PATCH, DELETE)\n- URLs represent resource hierarchies\n- Consistent naming conventions\n\n**HTTP Methods Semantics:**\n\n- `GET`: Retrieve resources (idempotent, safe)\n- `POST`: Create new resources\n- `PUT`: Replace entire resource (idempotent)\n- `PATCH`: Partial resource updates\n- `DELETE`: Remove resources (idempotent)\n\n### 2. GraphQL Design Principles\n\n**Schema-First Development**\n\n- Types define your domain model\n- Queries for reading data\n- Mutations for modifying data\n- Subscriptions for real-time updates\n\n**Query Structure:**\n\n- Clients request exactly what they need\n- Single endpoint, multiple operations\n- Strongly typed schema\n- Introspection built-in\n\n### 3. API Versioning Strategies\n\n**URL Versioning:**\n\n```\n/api/v1/users\n/api/v2/users\n```\n\n**Header Versioning:**\n\n```\nAccept: application/vnd.api+json; version=1\n```\n\n**Query Parameter Versioning:**\n\n```\n/api/users?version=1\n```\n\n## REST API Design Patterns\n\n### Pattern 1: Resource Collection Design\n\n```python\n# Good: Resource-oriented endpoints\nGET    /api/users              # List users (with pagination)\nPOST   /api/users              # Create user\nGET    /api/users/{id}         # Get specific user\nPUT    /api/users/{id}         # Replace user\nPATCH  /api/users/{id}         # Update user fields\nDELETE /api/users/{id}         # Delete user\n\n# Nested resources\nGET    /api/users/{id}/orders  # Get user's orders\nPOST   /api/users/{id}/orders  # Create order for user\n\n# Bad: Action-oriented endpoints (avoid)\nPOST   /api/createUser\nPOST   /api/getUserById\nPOST   /api/deleteUser\n```\n\n### Pattern 2: Pagination and Filtering\n\n```python\nfrom typing import List, Optional\nfrom pydantic import BaseModel, Field\n\nclass PaginationParams(BaseModel):\n    page: int = Field(1, ge=1, description=\"Page number\")\n    page_size: int = Field(20, ge=1, le=100, description=\"Items per page\")\n\nclass FilterParams(BaseModel):\n    status: Optional[str] = None\n    created_after: Optional[str] = None\n    search: Optional[str] = None\n\nclass PaginatedResponse(BaseModel):\n    items: List[dict]\n    total: int\n    page: int\n    page_size: int\n    pages: int\n\n    @property\n    def has_next(self) -> bool:\n        return self.page < self.pages\n\n    @property\n    def has_prev(self) -> bool:\n        return self.page > 1\n\n# FastAPI endpoint example\nfrom fastapi import FastAPI, Query, Depends\n\napp = FastAPI()\n\n@app.get(\"/api/users\", response_model=PaginatedResponse)\nasync def list_users(\n    page: int = Query(1, ge=1),\n    page_size: int = Query(20, ge=1, le=100),\n    status: Optional[str] = Query(None),\n    search: Optional[str] = Query(None)\n):\n    # Apply filters\n    query = build_query(status=status, search=search)\n\n    # Count total\n    total = await count_users(query)\n\n    # Fetch page\n    offset = (page - 1) * page_size\n    users = await fetch_users(query, limit=page_size, offset=offset)\n\n    return PaginatedResponse(\n        items=users,\n        total=total,\n        page=page,\n        page_size=page_size,\n        pages=(total + page_size - 1) // page_size\n    )\n```\n\n### Pattern 3: Error Handling and Status Codes\n\n```python\nfrom fastapi import HTTPException, status\nfrom pydantic import BaseModel\n\nclass ErrorResponse(BaseModel):\n    error: str\n    message: str\n    details: Optional[dict] = None\n    timestamp: str\n    path: str\n\nclass ValidationErrorDetail(BaseModel):\n    field: str\n    message: str\n    value: Any\n\n# Consistent error responses\nSTATUS_CODES = {\n    \"success\": 200,\n    \"created\": 201,\n    \"no_content\": 204,\n    \"bad_request\": 400,\n    \"unauthorized\": 401,\n    \"forbidden\": 403,\n    \"not_found\": 404,\n    \"conflict\": 409,\n    \"unprocessable\": 422,\n    \"internal_error\": 500\n}\n\ndef raise_not_found(resource: str, id: str):\n    raise HTTPException(\n        status_code=status.HTTP_404_NOT_FOUND,\n        detail={\n            \"error\": \"NotFound\",\n            \"message\": f\"{resource} not found\",\n            \"details\": {\"id\": id}\n        }\n    )\n\ndef raise_validation_error(errors: List[ValidationErrorDetail]):\n    raise HTTPException(\n        status_code=status.HTTP_422_UNPROCESSABLE_ENTITY,\n        detail={\n            \"error\": \"ValidationError\",\n            \"message\": \"Request validation failed\",\n            \"details\": {\"errors\": [e.dict() for e in errors]}\n        }\n    )\n\n# Example usage\n@app.get(\"/api/users/{user_id}\")\nasync def get_user(user_id: str):\n    user = await fetch_user(user_id)\n    if not user:\n        raise_not_found(\"User\", user_id)\n    return user\n```\n\n### Pattern 4: HATEOAS (Hypermedia as the Engine of Application State)\n\n```python\nclass UserResponse(BaseModel):\n    id: str\n    name: str\n    email: str\n    _links: dict\n\n    @classmethod\n    def from_user(cls, user: User, base_url: str):\n        return cls(\n            id=user.id,\n            name=user.name,\n            email=user.email,\n            _links={\n                \"self\": {\"href\": f\"{base_url}/api/users/{user.id}\"},\n                \"orders\": {\"href\": f\"{base_url}/api/users/{user.id}/orders\"},\n                \"update\": {\n                    \"href\": f\"{base_url}/api/users/{user.id}\",\n                    \"method\": \"PATCH\"\n                },\n                \"delete\": {\n                    \"href\": f\"{base_url}/api/users/{user.id}\",\n                    \"method\": \"DELETE\"\n                }\n            }\n        )\n```\n\n## GraphQL Design Patterns\n\n### Pattern 1: Schema Design\n\n```graphql\n# schema.graphql\n\n# Clear type definitions\ntype User {\n  id: ID!\n  email: String!\n  name: String!\n  createdAt: DateTime!\n\n  # Relationships\n  orders(first: Int = 20, after: String, status: OrderStatus): OrderConnection!\n\n  profile: UserProfile\n}\n\ntype Order {\n  id: ID!\n  status: OrderStatus!\n  total: Money!\n  items: [OrderItem!]!\n  createdAt: DateTime!\n\n  # Back-reference\n  user: User!\n}\n\n# Pagination pattern (Relay-style)\ntype OrderConnection {\n  edges: [OrderEdge!]!\n  pageInfo: PageInfo!\n  totalCount: Int!\n}\n\ntype OrderEdge {\n  node: Order!\n  cursor: String!\n}\n\ntype PageInfo {\n  hasNextPage: Boolean!\n  hasPreviousPage: Boolean!\n  startCursor: String\n  endCursor: String\n}\n\n# Enums for type safety\nenum OrderStatus {\n  PENDING\n  CONFIRMED\n  SHIPPED\n  DELIVERED\n  CANCELLED\n}\n\n# Custom scalars\nscalar DateTime\nscalar Money\n\n# Query root\ntype Query {\n  user(id: ID!): User\n  users(first: Int = 20, after: String, search: String): UserConnection!\n\n  order(id: ID!): Order\n}\n\n# Mutation root\ntype Mutation {\n  createUser(input: CreateUserInput!): CreateUserPayload!\n  updateUser(input: UpdateUserInput!): UpdateUserPayload!\n  deleteUser(id: ID!): DeleteUserPayload!\n\n  createOrder(input: CreateOrderInput!): CreateOrderPayload!\n}\n\n# Input types for mutations\ninput CreateUserInput {\n  email: String!\n  name: String!\n  password: String!\n}\n\n# Payload types for mutations\ntype CreateUserPayload {\n  user: User\n  errors: [Error!]\n}\n\ntype Error {\n  field: String\n  message: String!\n}\n```\n\n### Pattern 2: Resolver Design\n\n```python\nfrom typing import Optional, List\nfrom ariadne import QueryType, MutationType, ObjectType\nfrom dataclasses import dataclass\n\nquery = QueryType()\nmutation = MutationType()\nuser_type = ObjectType(\"User\")\n\n@query.field(\"user\")\nasync def resolve_user(obj, info, id: str) -> Optional[dict]:\n    \"\"\"Resolve single user by ID.\"\"\"\n    return await fetch_user_by_id(id)\n\n@query.field(\"users\")\nasync def resolve_users(\n    obj,\n    info,\n    first: int = 20,\n    after: Optional[str] = None,\n    search: Optional[str] = None\n) -> dict:\n    \"\"\"Resolve paginated user list.\"\"\"\n    # Decode cursor\n    offset = decode_cursor(after) if after else 0\n\n    # Fetch users\n    users = await fetch_users(\n        limit=first + 1,  # Fetch one extra to check hasNextPage\n        offset=offset,\n        search=search\n    )\n\n    # Pagination\n    has_next = len(users) > first\n    if has_next:\n        users = users[:first]\n\n    edges = [\n        {\n            \"node\": user,\n            \"cursor\": encode_cursor(offset + i)\n        }\n        for i, user in enumerate(users)\n    ]\n\n    return {\n        \"edges\": edges,\n        \"pageInfo\": {\n            \"hasNextPage\": has_next,\n            \"hasPreviousPage\": offset > 0,\n            \"startCursor\": edges[0][\"cursor\"] if edges else None,\n            \"endCursor\": edges[-1][\"cursor\"] if edges else None\n        },\n        \"totalCount\": await count_users(search=search)\n    }\n\n@user_type.field(\"orders\")\nasync def resolve_user_orders(user: dict, info, first: int = 20) -> dict:\n    \"\"\"Resolve user's orders (N+1 prevention with DataLoader).\"\"\"\n    # Use DataLoader to batch requests\n    loader = info.context[\"loaders\"][\"orders_by_user\"]\n    orders = await loader.load(user[\"id\"])\n\n    return paginate_orders(orders, first)\n\n@mutation.field(\"createUser\")\nasync def resolve_create_user(obj, info, input: dict) -> dict:\n    \"\"\"Create new user.\"\"\"\n    try:\n        # Validate input\n        validate_user_input(input)\n\n        # Create user\n        user = await create_user(\n            email=input[\"email\"],\n            name=input[\"name\"],\n            password=hash_password(input[\"password\"])\n        )\n\n        return {\n            \"user\": user,\n            \"errors\": []\n        }\n    except ValidationError as e:\n        return {\n            \"user\": None,\n            \"errors\": [{\"field\": e.field, \"message\": e.message}]\n        }\n```\n\n### Pattern 3: DataLoader (N+1 Problem Prevention)\n\n```python\nfrom aiodataloader import DataLoader\nfrom typing import List, Optional\n\nclass UserLoader(DataLoader):\n    \"\"\"Batch load users by ID.\"\"\"\n\n    async def batch_load_fn(self, user_ids: List[str]) -> List[Optional[dict]]:\n        \"\"\"Load multiple users in single query.\"\"\"\n        users = await fetch_users_by_ids(user_ids)\n\n        # Map results back to input order\n        user_map = {user[\"id\"]: user for user in users}\n        return [user_map.get(user_id) for user_id in user_ids]\n\nclass OrdersByUserLoader(DataLoader):\n    \"\"\"Batch load orders by user ID.\"\"\"\n\n    async def batch_load_fn(self, user_ids: List[str]) -> List[List[dict]]:\n        \"\"\"Load orders for multiple users in single query.\"\"\"\n        orders = await fetch_orders_by_user_ids(user_ids)\n\n        # Group orders by user_id\n        orders_by_user = {}\n        for order in orders:\n            user_id = order[\"user_id\"]\n            if user_id not in orders_by_user:\n                orders_by_user[user_id] = []\n            orders_by_user[user_id].append(order)\n\n        # Return in input order\n        return [orders_by_user.get(user_id, []) for user_id in user_ids]\n\n# Context setup\ndef create_context():\n    return {\n        \"loaders\": {\n            \"user\": UserLoader(),\n            \"orders_by_user\": OrdersByUserLoader()\n        }\n    }\n```\n\n## Best Practices\n\n### REST APIs\n\n1. **Consistent Naming**: Use plural nouns for collections (`/users`, not `/user`)\n2. **Stateless**: Each request contains all necessary information\n3. **Use HTTP Status Codes Correctly**: 2xx success, 4xx client errors, 5xx server errors\n4. **Version Your API**: Plan for breaking changes from day one\n5. **Pagination**: Always paginate large collections\n6. **Rate Limiting**: Protect your API with rate limits\n7. **Documentation**: Use OpenAPI/Swagger for interactive docs\n\n### GraphQL APIs\n\n1. **Schema First**: Design schema before writing resolvers\n2. **Avoid N+1**: Use DataLoaders for efficient data fetching\n3. **Input Validation**: Validate at schema and resolver levels\n4. **Error Handling**: Return structured errors in mutation payloads\n5. **Pagination**: Use cursor-based pagination (Relay spec)\n6. **Deprecation**: Use `@deprecated` directive for gradual migration\n7. **Monitoring**: Track query complexity and execution time\n\n## Common Pitfalls\n\n- **Over-fetching/Under-fetching (REST)**: Fixed in GraphQL but requires DataLoaders\n- **Breaking Changes**: Version APIs or use deprecation strategies\n- **Inconsistent Error Formats**: Standardize error responses\n- **Missing Rate Limits**: APIs without limits are vulnerable to abuse\n- **Poor Documentation**: Undocumented APIs frustrate developers\n- **Ignoring HTTP Semantics**: POST for idempotent operations breaks expectations\n- **Tight Coupling**: API structure shouldn't mirror database schema\n\n## Resources\n\n- **references/rest-best-practices.md**: Comprehensive REST API design guide\n- **references/graphql-schema-design.md**: GraphQL schema patterns and anti-patterns\n- **references/api-versioning-strategies.md**: Versioning approaches and migration paths\n- |\n|-----------|----------------|\n| **Feature isolation** | Each feature in `features/` with its own components, hooks, actions |\n| **Server/Client separation** | Server-only code in `server/`, Use when writing scenarios, use cases, user journeys, or storyboards. Triggers on: assistive technology, AT, screen reader, switch, voice control, magnifier, braille display, head pointer, eye tracking, how people use our product, usage scenario, use case.\"\n---\n\n# Assistive Technology Scenarios\n\nWrite scenarios that show real people using real assistive technology\nto complete real tasks ÔÇö so the team designs for actual usage, not\nimagined ideal conditions.\n\n## Why This Matters\n\nIf your scenarios only describe people using a mouse and a large\nmonitor, your design will only work for people using a mouse and a\nlarge monitor. Scenarios shape what teams consider. What they consider,\nthey design for.\n\n## Common Assistive Technologies\n\n### Vision\n- **Screen reader** (JAWS, NVDA, VoiceOver, TalkBack): reads content\n  aloud, navigates by headings, links, and landmarks via keyboard\n- **Screen magnifier** (ZoomText, built-in zoom): enlarges a portion\n  of the screen, user sees only a fraction at a time\n- **Braille display**: converts text to braille in real time, used\n  alongside a screen reader\n- **High contrast mode**: system-level colour override for readability\n\n### Motor\n- **Switch device**: one or two buttons that scan through options ÔÇö\n  much slower than keyboard, every extra step matters\n- **Voice control** (Dragon, Voice Control, Voice Access): speaks\n  commands to navigate and type\n- **Head pointer / eye tracking**: cursor controlled by head movement\n  or eye gaze ÔÇö precision is limited\n- **Mouth stick / stylus**: physical pointer for touchscreens or\n  keyboards\n\n### Hearing\n- **Captions**: real-time or pre-recorded text for audio content\n- **Visual alerts**: flashing screen or visual indicator replacing\n  audio notifications\n- **Hearing loop / Bluetooth**: direct audio to hearing aids\n\n### Cognitive\n- **Text-to-speech**: reads content aloud (not a screen reader ÔÇö\n  the user can see the screen but processes better by hearing)\n- **Reading ruler / line guide**: highlights one line at a time\n- **Simplified view**: browser extensions or settings that strip\n  clutter\n- **Password managers**: reduce memory burden for authentication\n\n## How to Write AT Scenarios\n\n### Include the Setup\nDon't just name the disability. Describe the person, their technology,\nand their environment.\n\n\"Priya is a data analyst who uses JAWS screen reader with Firefox on\na Windows laptop. She's fast with keyboard shortcuts and has two\nmonitors ÔÇö one for her spreadsheets, one for the tool she's reviewing.\nShe's at her desk with noise-cancelling headphones.\"\n\n### Show the Interaction Step by Step\nDescribe how the person actually moves through the interface with\ntheir technology:\n\n\"Priya tabs to the navigation menu. JAWS announces 'Navigation,\n5 items.' She presses the down arrow to find 'Reports' and presses\nEnter. The page loads and JAWS announces the H1: 'Monthly Reports.'\nShe presses H to jump through headings until she finds 'March 2026.'\"\n\n### Show Where It Breaks\nThe scenario should surface design failures:\n\n\"Priya reaches the data table but JAWS announces each cell without\ncolumn headers. She hears '42,500' but doesn't know which metric or\nwhich month. She tabs past the table looking for a text summary but\nthere isn't one.\"\n\n## Scenario Coverage\n\nFor any product, write scenarios for at least:\n- One screen reader user completing the core task\n- One keyboard-only user completing the core task\n- One user with low vision using magnification\n- One user with a cognitive difference\n- One user in a situational impairment context\n\n## Template\n```\nName: [Name]\nRole/context: [Who they are beyond their disability]\nTechnology: [Devices, AT, browser, settings]\nEnvironment: [Where, when, what else is happening]\nTask: [What they're trying to accomplish]\nSteps: [How they interact, step by step, using their AT]\nPain points: [Where the design fails or creates friction]\nSuccess: [What a good outcome looks like for this person]\n```\n\n## Assessment Questions\n\n1. Does the scenario name specific assistive technology, not just\n   the disability?\n2. Does it describe step-by-step interaction, not just the goal?\n3. Does it surface specific design failures?\n4. Would a developer reading this know what to fix?\n", "sector": "procedural", "salience": 1.0}, {"id": "skill_69", "label": "audit-stories", "group": "industrial", "content": "---\nname: audit-stories\ndescription: \"Audit user stories for disability inclusion. Chains: inclusive-user-stories, edge-case-identification. Use when reviewing an existing backlog, sprint, or set of user stories to check whether they account for diverse abilities.\"\n---\n\n# Audit User Stories for Inclusion\n\nReview an existing set of user stories and identify where disability\nand diverse abilities have been overlooked ÔÇö then add the missing\ncriteria without creating separate accessibility tickets.\n\n## Process\n\n### Step 1: Gather Stories\nCollect the user stories to audit. This could be a sprint backlog,\na feature's full story set, or an epic.\n\n### Step 2: Check Each Story\nUsing **inclusive-user-stories**, review each story's acceptance\ncriteria for:\n- Keyboard operability\n- Screen reader support\n- Visual accessibility (contrast, colour independence, zoom)\n- Motor accessibility (target size, timing, precision)\n- Cognitive accessibility (plain language, error recovery, undo)\n\nFlag any story where one or more of these are missing.\n\n### Step 3: Check for Assumption Bias\nUsing **edge-case-identification**, for each story ask:\n- What input method does this assume?\n- What sensory ability does this assume?\n- What cognitive capacity does this assume?\n- What context does this assume?\n- What \"edge cases\" have been dismissed that are daily reality\n  for some users?\n\n### Step 4: Add Missing Criteria\nFor each flagged story, write the specific acceptance criteria that\nneed to be added. Don't create separate stories ÔÇö add directly to\nthe existing ones.\n\n## Output\n\nDeliver an audit report:\n\n1. **Summary** ÔÇö number of stories reviewed, number with gaps,\n   most common gap type\n2. **Story-by-story findings** ÔÇö each story with its missing\n   criteria listed\n3. **Added criteria** ÔÇö the specific acceptance criteria to add\n   to each story, ready to paste into the backlog\n4. **Patterns** ÔÇö recurring gaps that suggest a systemic issue\n   (e.g., \"no story in this sprint mentions keyboard access\")\n", "sector": "procedural", "salience": 1.0}, {"id": "skill_70", "label": "backend-dev-guidelines", "group": "industrial", "content": "---\nversion: 4.1.0-fractal\nname: backend-dev-guidelines\ndescription: Opinionated backend development standards for Node.js + Express + TypeScript microservices. Covers layered architecture, BaseController pattern, dependency injection, Prisma repositories, Zod validation, unifiedConfig, Sentry error tracking, async safety, and testing discipline.\n---\n\n# Backend Development Guidelines\n\n**(Node.js ┬À Express ┬À TypeScript ┬À Microservices)**\n\nYou are a **senior backend engineer** operating production-grade services under strict architectural and reliability constraints.\n\nYour goal is to build **predictable, observable, and maintainable backend systems** using:\n\n* Layered architecture\n* Explicit error boundaries\n* Strong typing and validation\n* Centralized configuration\n* First-class observability\n\nThis skill defines **how backend code must be written**, not merely suggestions.\n\n---\n\n## 1. Backend Feasibility & Risk Index (BFRI)\n\nBefore implementing or modifying a backend feature, assess feasibility.\n\n### BFRI Dimensions (1ÔÇô5)\n\n| Dimension                     | Question                                                         |\n| ----------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------- |\n| **Architectural Fit**         | Does this follow routes ÔåÆ controllers ÔåÆ services ÔåÆ repositories? |\n| **Business Logic Complexity** | How complex is the domain logic?                                 |\n| **Data Risk**                 | Does this affect critical data paths or transactions?            |\n| **Operational Risk**          | Does this impact auth, billing, messaging, or infra?             |\n| **Testability**               | Can this be reliably unit + integration tested?                  |\n\n### Score Formula\n\n```\nBFRI = (Architectural Fit + Testability) ÔêÆ (Complexity + Data Risk + Operational Risk)\n```\n\n**Range:** `-10 ÔåÆ +10`\n\n### Interpretation\n\n| BFRI     | Meaning   | Action                 |\n| -------- | --------- | ---------------------- |\n| **6ÔÇô10** | Safe      | Proceed                |\n| **3ÔÇô5**  | Moderate  | Add tests + monitoring |\n| **0ÔÇô2**  | Risky     | Refactor or isolate    |\n| **< 0**  | Dangerous | Redesign before coding |\n\n---\n\n## 2. When to Use This Skill\n\nAutomatically applies when working on:\n\n* Routes, controllers, services, repositories\n* Express middleware\n* Prisma database access\n* Zod validation\n* Sentry error tracking\n* Configuration management\n* Backend refactors or migrations\n\n---\n\n## 3. Core Architecture Doctrine (Non-Negotiable)\n\n### 1. Layered Architecture Is Mandatory\n\n```\nRoutes ÔåÆ Controllers ÔåÆ Services ÔåÆ Repositories ÔåÆ Database\n```\n\n* No layer skipping\n* No cross-layer leakage\n* Each layer has **one responsibility**\n\n---\n\n### 2. Routes Only Route\n\n```ts\n// ÔØî NEVER\nrouter.post('/create', async (req, res) => {\n  await prisma.user.create(...);\n});\n\n// Ô£à ALWAYS\nrouter.post('/create', (req, res) =>\n  userController.create(req, res)\n);\n```\n\nRoutes must contain **zero business logic**.\n\n---\n\n### 3. Controllers Coordinate, Services Decide\n\n* Controllers:\n\n  * Parse request\n  * Call services\n  * Handle response formatting\n  * Handle errors via BaseController\n\n* Services:\n\n  * Contain business rules\n  * Are framework-agnostic\n  * Use DI\n  * Are unit-testable\n\n---\n\n### 4. All Controllers Extend `BaseController`\n\n```ts\nexport class UserController extends BaseController {\n  async getUser(req: Request, res: Response): Promise<void> {\n    try {\n      const user = await this.userService.getById(req.params.id);\n      this.handleSuccess(res, user);\n    } catch (error) {\n      this.handleError(error, res, 'getUser');\n    }\n  }\n}\n```\n\nNo raw `res.json` calls outside BaseController helpers.\n\n---\n\n### 5. All Errors Go to Sentry\n\n```ts\ncatch (error) {\n  Sentry.captureException(error);\n  throw error;\n}\n```\n\nÔØî `console.log`\nÔØî silent failures\nÔØî swallowed errors\n\n---\n\n### 6. unifiedConfig Is the Only Config Source\n\n```ts\n// ÔØî NEVER\nprocess.env.JWT_SECRET;\n\n// Ô£à ALWAYS\nimport { config } from '@/config/unifiedConfig';\nconfig.auth.jwtSecret;\n```\n\n---\n\n### 7. Validate All External Input with Zod\n\n* Request bodies\n* Query params\n* Route params\n* Webhook payloads\n\n```ts\nconst schema = z.object({\n  email: z.string().email(),\n});\n\nconst input = schema.parse(req.body);\n```\n\nNo validation = bug.\n\n---\n\n## 4. Directory Structure (Canonical)\n\n```\nsrc/\nÔö£ÔöÇÔöÇ config/              # unifiedConfig\nÔö£ÔöÇÔöÇ controllers/         # BaseController + controllers\nÔö£ÔöÇÔöÇ services/            # Business logic\nÔö£ÔöÇÔöÇ repositories/        # Prisma access\nÔö£ÔöÇÔöÇ routes/              # Express routes\nÔö£ÔöÇÔöÇ middleware/          # Auth, validation, errors\nÔö£ÔöÇÔöÇ validators/          # Zod schemas\nÔö£ÔöÇÔöÇ types/               # Shared types\nÔö£ÔöÇÔöÇ utils/               # Helpers\nÔö£ÔöÇÔöÇ tests/               # Unit + integration tests\nÔö£ÔöÇÔöÇ instrument.ts        # Sentry (FIRST IMPORT)\nÔö£ÔöÇÔöÇ app.ts               # Express app\nÔööÔöÇÔöÇ server.ts            # HTTP server\n```\n\n---\n\n## 5. Naming Conventions (Strict)\n\n| Layer      | Convention                |\n| ---------- | ------------------------- |\n| Controller | `PascalCaseController.ts` |\n| Service    | `camelCaseService.ts`     |\n| Repository | `PascalCaseRepository.ts` |\n| Routes     | `camelCaseRoutes.ts`      |\n| Validators | `camelCase.schema.ts`     |\n\n---\n\n## 6. Dependency Injection Rules\n\n* Services receive dependencies via constructor\n* No importing repositories directly inside controllers\n* Enables mocking and testing\n\n```ts\nexport class UserService {\n  constructor(\n    private readonly userRepository: UserRepository\n  ) {}\n}\n```\n\n---\n\n## 7. Prisma & Repository Rules\n\n* Prisma client **never used directly in controllers**\n* Repositories:\n\n  * Encapsulate queries\n  * Handle transactions\n  * Expose intent-based methods\n\n```ts\nawait userRepository.findActiveUsers();\n```\n\n---\n\n## 8. Async & Error Handling\n\n### asyncErrorWrapper Required\n\nAll async route handlers must be wrapped.\n\n```ts\nrouter.get(\n  '/users',\n  asyncErrorWrapper((req, res) =>\n    controller.list(req, res)\n  )\n);\n```\n\nNo unhandled promise rejections.\n\n---\n\n## 9. Observability & Monitoring\n\n### Required\n\n* Sentry error tracking\n* Sentry performance tracing\n* Structured logs (where applicable)\n\nEvery critical path must be observable.\n\n---\n\n## 10. Testing Discipline\n\n### Required Tests\n\n* **Unit tests** for services\n* **Integration tests** for routes\n* **Repository tests** for complex queries\n\n```ts\ndescribe('UserService', () => {\n  it('creates a user', async () => {\n    expect(user).toBeDefined();\n  });\n});\n```\n\nNo tests ÔåÆ no merge.\n\n---\n\n## 11. Anti-Patterns (Immediate Rejection)\n\nÔØî Business logic in routes\nÔØî Skipping service layer\nÔØî Direct Prisma in controllers\nÔØî Missing validation\nÔØî process.env usage\nÔØî console.log instead of Sentry\nÔØî Untested business logic\n\n---\n\n## 12. Integration With Other Skills\n\n* **frontend-dev-guidelines** ÔåÆ API contract alignment\n* **error-tracking** ÔåÆ Sentry standards\n* **database-verification** ÔåÆ Schema correctness\n* **analytics-tracking** ÔåÆ Event pipelines\n* **skill-developer** ÔåÆ Skill governance\n\n---\n\n## 13. Operator Validation Checklist\n\nBefore finalizing backend work:\n\n* [ ] BFRI ÔëÑ 3\n* [ ] Layered architecture respected\n* [ ] Input validated\n* [ ] Errors captured in Sentry\n* [ ] unifiedConfig used\n* [ ] Tests written\n* [ ] No anti-patterns present\n\n---\n\n## 14. Skill Status\n\n**Status:** Stable ┬À Enforceable ┬À Production-grade\n**Intended Use:** Long-lived Node.js microservices with real traffic and real risk\n---\n\n\n## ­ƒºá Knowledge Modules (Fractal Skills)\n\n### 1. [validation-patterns](./sub-skills/validation-patterns.md)\n", "sector": "procedural", "salience": 1.0}, {"id": "skill_71", "label": "sub-skills", "group": "industrial", "content": "# Architecture Overview - Backend Services\n\nComplete guide to the layered architecture pattern used in backend microservices.\n\n## Table of Contents\n\n- [Layered Architecture Pattern](#layered-architecture-pattern)\n- [Request Lifecycle](#request-lifecycle)\n- [Service Comparison](#service-comparison)\n- [Directory Structure Rationale](#directory-structure-rationale)\n- [Module Organization](#module-organization)\n- [Separation of Concerns](#separation-of-concerns)\n\n---\n\n## Layered Architecture Pattern\n\n### The Four Layers\n\n```\nÔöîÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÉ\nÔöé         HTTP Request                Ôöé\nÔööÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔö¼ÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÿ\n                Ôåô\nÔöîÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÉ\nÔöé  Layer 1: ROUTES                    Ôöé\nÔöé  - Route definitions only           Ôöé\nÔöé  - Middleware registration          Ôöé\nÔöé  - Delegate to controllers          Ôöé\nÔöé  - NO business logic                Ôöé\nÔööÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔö¼ÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÿ\n                Ôåô\nÔöîÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÉ\nÔöé  Layer 2: CONTROLLERS               Ôöé\nÔöé  - Request/response handling        Ôöé\nÔöé  - Input validation                 Ôöé\nÔöé  - Call services                    Ôöé\nÔöé  - Format responses                 Ôöé\nÔöé  - Error handling                   Ôöé\nÔööÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔö¼ÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÿ\n                Ôåô\nÔöîÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÉ\nÔöé  Layer 3: SERVICES                  Ôöé\nÔöé  - Business logic                   Ôöé\nÔöé  - Orchestration                    Ôöé\nÔöé  - Call repositories                Ôöé\nÔöé  - No HTTP knowledge                Ôöé\nÔööÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔö¼ÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÿ\n                Ôåô\nÔöîÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÉ\nÔöé  Layer 4: REPOSITORIES              Ôöé\nÔöé  - Data access abstraction          Ôöé\nÔöé  - Prisma operations                Ôöé\nÔöé  - Query optimization               Ôöé\nÔöé  - Caching                          Ôöé\nÔööÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔö¼ÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÿ\n                Ôåô\nÔöîÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÉ\nÔöé         Database (MySQL)            Ôöé\nÔööÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÇÔöÿ\n```\n\n### Why This Architecture?\n\n**Testability:**\n- Each layer can be tested independently\n- Easy to mock dependencies\n- Clear test boundaries\n\n**Maintainability:**\n- Changes isolated to specific layers\n- Business logic separate from HTTP concerns\n- Easy to locate bugs\n\n**Reusability:**\n- Services can be used by routes, cron jobs, scripts\n- Repositories hide database implementation\n- Business logic not tied to HTTP\n\n**Scalability:**\n- Easy to add new endpoints\n- Clear patterns to follow\n- Consistent structure\n\n---\n\n## Request Lifecycle\n\n### Complete Flow Example\n\n```typescript\n1. HTTP POST /api/users\n   Ôåô\n2. Express matches route in userRoutes.ts\n   Ôåô\n3. Middleware chain executes:\n   - SSOMiddleware.verifyLoginStatus (authentication)\n   - auditMiddleware (context tracking)\n   Ôåô\n4. Route handler delegates to controller:\n   router.post('/users', (req, res) => userController.create(req, res))\n   Ôåô\n5. Controller validates and calls service:\n   - Validate input with Zod\n   - Call userService.create(data)\n   - Handle success/error\n   Ôåô\n6. Service executes business logic:\n   - Check business rules\n   - Call userRepository.create(data)\n   - Return result\n   Ôåô\n7. Repository performs database operation:\n   - PrismaService.main.user.create({ data })\n   - Handle database errors\n   - Return created user\n   Ôåô\n8. Response flows back:\n   Repository ÔåÆ Service ÔåÆ Controller ÔåÆ Express ÔåÆ Client\n```\n\n### Middleware Execution Order\n\n**Critical:** Middleware executes in registration order\n\n```typescript\napp.use(Sentry.Handlers.requestHandler());  // 1. Sentry tracing (FIRST)\napp.use(express.json());                     // 2. Body parsing\napp.use(express.urlencoded({ extended: true })); // 3. URL encoding\napp.use(cookieParser());                     // 4. Cookie parsing\napp.use(SSOMiddleware.initialize());         // 5. Auth initialization\n// ... routes registered here\napp.use(auditMiddleware);                    // 6. Audit (if global)\napp.use(errorBoundary);                      // 7. Error handler (LAST)\napp.use(Sentry.Handlers.errorHandler());     // 8. Sentry errors (LAST)\n```\n\n**Rule:** Error handlers must be registered AFTER routes!\n\n---\n\n## Service Comparison\n\n### Email Service (Mature Pattern Ô£à)\n\n**Strengths:**\n- Comprehensive BaseController with Sentry integration\n- Clean route delegation (no business logic in routes)\n- Consistent dependency injection pattern\n- Good middleware organization\n- Type-safe throughout\n- Excellent error handling\n\n**Example Structure:**\n```\nemail/src/\nÔö£ÔöÇÔöÇ controllers/\nÔöé   Ôö£ÔöÇÔöÇ BaseController.ts          Ô£à Excellent template\nÔöé   Ôö£ÔöÇÔöÇ NotificationController.ts  Ô£à Extends BaseController\nÔöé   ÔööÔöÇÔöÇ EmailController.ts         Ô£à Clean patterns\nÔö£ÔöÇÔöÇ routes/\nÔöé   Ôö£ÔöÇÔöÇ notificationRoutes.ts      Ô£à Clean delegation\nÔöé   ÔööÔöÇÔöÇ emailRoutes.ts             Ô£à No business logic\nÔö£ÔöÇÔöÇ services/\nÔöé   Ôö£ÔöÇÔöÇ NotificationService.ts     Ô£à Dependency injection\nÔöé   ÔööÔöÇÔöÇ BatchingService.ts         Ô£à Clear responsibility\nÔööÔöÇÔöÇ middleware/\n    Ôö£ÔöÇÔöÇ errorBoundary.ts           Ô£à Comprehensive\n    ÔööÔöÇÔöÇ DevImpersonationSSOMiddleware.ts\n```\n\n**Use as template** for new services!\n\n### Form Service (Transitioning ÔÜá´©Å)\n\n**Strengths:**\n- Excellent workflow architecture (event sourcing)\n- Good Sentry integration\n- Innovative audit middleware (AsyncLocalStorage)\n- Comprehensive permission system\n\n**Weaknesses:**\n- Some routes have 200+ lines of business logic\n- Inconsistent controller naming\n- Direct process.env usage (60+ occurrences)\n- Minimal repository pattern usage\n\n**Example:**\n```\nform/src/\nÔö£ÔöÇÔöÇ routes/\nÔöé   Ôö£ÔöÇÔöÇ responseRoutes.ts          ÔØî Business logic in routes\nÔöé   ÔööÔöÇÔöÇ proxyRoutes.ts             Ô£à Good validation pattern\nÔö£ÔöÇÔöÇ controllers/\nÔöé   Ôö£ÔöÇÔöÇ formController.ts          ÔÜá´©Å Lowercase naming\nÔöé   ÔööÔöÇÔöÇ UserProfileController.ts   Ô£à PascalCase naming\nÔö£ÔöÇÔöÇ workflow/                      Ô£à Excellent architecture!\nÔöé   Ôö£ÔöÇÔöÇ core/\nÔöé   Ôöé   Ôö£ÔöÇÔöÇ WorkflowEngineV3.ts   Ô£à Event sourcing\nÔöé   Ôöé   ÔööÔöÇÔöÇ DryRunWrapper.ts      Ô£à Innovative\nÔöé   ÔööÔöÇÔöÇ services/\nÔööÔöÇÔöÇ middleware/\n    ÔööÔöÇÔöÇ auditMiddleware.ts         Ô£à AsyncLocalStorage pattern\n```\n\n**Learn from:** workflow/, middleware/auditMiddleware.ts\n**Avoid:** responseRoutes.ts, direct process.env\n\n---\n\n## Directory Structure Rationale\n\n### Controllers Directory\n\n**Purpose:** Handle HTTP request/response concerns\n\n**Contents:**\n- `BaseController.ts` - Base class with common methods\n- `{Feature}Controller.ts` - Feature-specific controllers\n\n**Naming:** PascalCase + Controller\n\n**Responsibilities:**\n- Parse request parameters\n- Validate input (Zod)\n- Call appropriate service methods\n- Format responses\n- Handle errors (via BaseController)\n- Set HTTP status codes\n\n### Services Directory\n\n**Purpose:** Business logic and orchestration\n\n**Contents:**\n- `{feature}Service.ts` - Feature business logic\n\n**Naming:** camelCase + Service (or PascalCase + Service)\n\n**Responsibilities:**\n- Implement business rules\n- Orchestrate multiple repositories\n- Transaction management\n- Business validations\n- No HTTP knowledge (Request/Response types)\n\n### Repositories Directory\n\n**Purpose:** Data access abstraction\n\n**Contents:**\n- `{Entity}Repository.ts` - Database operations for entity\n\n**Naming:** PascalCase + Repository\n\n**Responsibilities:**\n- Prisma query operations\n- Query optimization\n- Database error handling\n- Caching layer\n- Hide Prisma implementation details\n\n**Current Gap:** Only 1 repository exists (WorkflowRepository)\n\n### Routes Directory\n\n**Purpose:** Route registration ONLY\n\n**Contents:**\n- `{feature}Routes.ts` - Express router for feature\n\n**Naming:** camelCase + Routes\n\n**Responsibilities:**\n- Register routes with Express\n- Apply middleware\n- Delegate to controllers\n- **NO business logic!**\n\n### Middleware Directory\n\n**Purpose:** Cross-cutting concerns\n\n**Contents:**\n- Authentication middleware\n- Audit middleware\n- Error boundaries\n- Validation middleware\n- Custom middleware\n\n**Naming:** camelCase\n\n**Types:**\n- Request processing (before handler)\n- Response processing (after handler)\n- Error handling (error boundary)\n\n### Config Directory\n\n**Purpose:** Configuration management\n\n**Contents:**\n- `unifiedConfig.ts` - Type-safe configuration\n- Environment-specific configs\n\n**Pattern:** Single source of truth\n\n### Types Directory\n\n**Purpose:** TypeScript type definitions\n\n**Contents:**\n- `{feature}.types.ts` - Feature-specific types\n- DTOs (Data Transfer Objects)\n- Request/Response types\n- Domain models\n\n---\n\n## Module Organization\n\n### Feature-Based Organization\n\nFor large features, use subdirectories:\n\n```\nsrc/workflow/\nÔö£ÔöÇÔöÇ core/              # Core engine\nÔö£ÔöÇÔöÇ services/          # Workflow-specific services\nÔö£ÔöÇÔöÇ actions/           # System actions\nÔö£ÔöÇÔöÇ models/            # Domain models\nÔö£ÔöÇÔöÇ validators/        # Workflow validation\nÔööÔöÇÔöÇ utils/             # Workflow utilities\n```\n\n**When to use:**\n- Feature has 5+ files\n- Clear sub-domains exist\n- Logical grouping improves clarity\n\n### Flat Organization\n\nFor simple features:\n\n```\nsrc/\nÔö£ÔöÇÔöÇ controllers/UserController.ts\nÔö£ÔöÇÔöÇ services/userService.ts\nÔö£ÔöÇÔöÇ routes/userRoutes.ts\nÔööÔöÇÔöÇ repositories/UserRepository.ts\n```\n\n**When to use:**\n- Simple features (< 5 files)\n- No clear sub-domains\n- Flat structure is clearer\n\n---\n\n## Separation of Concerns\n\n### What Goes Where\n\n**Routes Layer:**\n- Ô£à Route definitions\n- Ô£à Middleware registration\n- Ô£à Controller delegation\n- ÔØî Business logic\n- ÔØî Database operations\n- ÔØî Validation logic (should be in validator or controller)\n\n**Controllers Layer:**\n- Ô£à Request parsing (params, body, query)\n- Ô£à Input validation (Zod)\n- Ô£à Service calls\n- Ô£à Response formatting\n- Ô£à Error handling\n- ÔØî Business logic\n- ÔØî Database operations\n\n**Services Layer:**\n- Ô£à Business logic\n- Ô£à Business rules enforcement\n- Ô£à Orchestration (multiple repos)\n- Ô£à Transaction management\n- ÔØî HTTP concerns (Request/Response)\n- ÔØî Direct Prisma calls (use repositories)\n\n**Repositories Layer:**\n- Ô£à Prisma operations\n- Ô£à Query construction\n- Ô£à Database error handling\n- Ô£à Caching\n- ÔØî Business logic\n- ÔØî HTTP concerns\n\n### Example: User Creation\n\n**Route:**\n```typescript\nrouter.post('/users',\n    SSOMiddleware.verifyLoginStatus,\n    auditMiddleware,\n    (req, res) => userController.create(req, res)\n);\n```\n\n**Controller:**\n```typescript\nasync create(req: Request, res: Response): Promise<void> {\n    try {\n        const validated = createUserSchema.parse(req.body);\n        const user = await this.userService.create(validated);\n        this.handleSuccess(res, user, 'User created');\n    } catch (error) {\n        this.handleError(error, res, 'create');\n    }\n}\n```\n\n**Service:**\n```typescript\nasync create(data: CreateUserDTO): Promise<User> {\n    // Business rule: check if email already exists\n    const existing = await this.userRepository.findByEmail(data.email);\n    if (existing) throw new ConflictError('Email already exists');\n\n    // Create user\n    return await this.userRepository.create(data);\n}\n```\n\n**Repository:**\n```typescript\nasync create(data: CreateUserDTO): Promise<User> {\n    return PrismaService.main.user.create({ data });\n}\n\nasync findByEmail(email: string): Promise<User | null> {\n    return PrismaService.main.user.findUnique({ where: { email } });\n}\n```\n\n**Notice:** Each layer has clear, distinct responsibilities!\n\n---\n\n**Related Files:**\n- [SKILL.md](SKILL.md) - Main guide\n- [routing-and-controllers.md](routing-and-controllers.md) - Routes and controllers details\n- [services-and-repositories.md](services-and-repositories.md) - Service and repository patterns\n", "sector": "procedural", "salience": 1.0}, {"id": "skill_72", "label": "sub-skills", "group": "industrial", "content": "# Async Patterns and Error Handling\n\nComplete guide to async/await patterns and custom error handling.\n\n## Table of Contents\n\n- [Async/Await Best Practices](#asyncawait-best-practices)\n- [Promise Error Handling](#promise-error-handling)\n- [Custom Error Types](#custom-error-types)\n- [asyncErrorWrapper Utility](#asyncerrorwrapper-utility)\n- [Error Propagation](#error-propagation)\n- [Common Async Pitfalls](#common-async-pitfalls)\n\n---\n\n## Async/Await Best Practices\n\n### Always Use Try-Catch\n\n```typescript\n// ÔØî NEVER: Unhandled async errors\nasync function fetchData() {\n    const data = await database.query(); // If throws, unhandled!\n    return data;\n}\n\n// Ô£à ALWAYS: Wrap in try-catch\nasync function fetchData() {\n    try {\n        const data = await database.query();\n        return data;\n    } catch (error) {\n        Sentry.captureException(error);\n        throw error;\n    }\n}\n```\n\n### Avoid .then() Chains\n\n```typescript\n// ÔØî AVOID: Promise chains\nfunction processData() {\n    return fetchData()\n        .then(data => transform(data))\n        .then(transformed => save(transformed))\n        .catch(error => {\n            console.error(error);\n        });\n}\n\n// Ô£à PREFER: Async/await\nasync function processData() {\n    try {\n        const data = await fetchData();\n        const transformed = await transform(data);\n        return await save(transformed);\n    } catch (error) {\n        Sentry.captureException(error);\n        throw error;\n    }\n}\n```\n\n---\n\n## Promise Error Handling\n\n### Parallel Operations\n\n```typescript\n// Ô£à Handle errors in Promise.all\ntry {\n    const [users, profiles, settings] = await Promise.all([\n        userService.getAll(),\n        profileService.getAll(),\n        settingsService.getAll(),\n    ]);\n} catch (error) {\n    // One failure fails all\n    Sentry.captureException(error);\n    throw error;\n}\n\n// Ô£à Handle errors individually with Promise.allSettled\nconst results = await Promise.allSettled([\n    userService.getAll(),\n    profileService.getAll(),\n    settingsService.getAll(),\n]);\n\nresults.forEach((result, index) => {\n    if (result.status === 'rejected') {\n        Sentry.captureException(result.reason, {\n            tags: { operation: ['users', 'profiles', 'settings'][index] }\n        });\n    }\n});\n```\n\n---\n\n## Custom Error Types\n\n### Define Custom Errors\n\n```typescript\n// Base error class\nexport class AppError extends Error {\n    constructor(\n        message: string,\n        public code: string,\n        public statusCode: number,\n        public isOperational: boolean = true\n    ) {\n        super(message);\n        this.name = this.constructor.name;\n        Error.captureStackTrace(this, this.constructor);\n    }\n}\n\n// Specific error types\nexport class ValidationError extends AppError {\n    constructor(message: string) {\n        super(message, 'VALIDATION_ERROR', 400);\n    }\n}\n\nexport class NotFoundError extends AppError {\n    constructor(message: string) {\n        super(message, 'NOT_FOUND', 404);\n    }\n}\n\nexport class ForbiddenError extends AppError {\n    constructor(message: string) {\n        super(message, 'FORBIDDEN', 403);\n    }\n}\n\nexport class ConflictError extends AppError {\n    constructor(message: string) {\n        super(message, 'CONFLICT', 409);\n    }\n}\n```\n\n### Usage\n\n```typescript\n// Throw specific errors\nif (!user) {\n    throw new NotFoundError('User not found');\n}\n\nif (user.age < 18) {\n    throw new ValidationError('User must be 18+');\n}\n\n// Error boundary handles them\nfunction errorBoundary(error, req, res, next) {\n    if (error instanceof AppError) {\n        return res.status(error.statusCode).json({\n            error: {\n                message: error.message,\n                code: error.code\n            }\n        });\n    }\n\n    // Unknown error\n    Sentry.captureException(error);\n    res.status(500).json({ error: { message: 'Internal server error' } });\n}\n```\n\n---\n\n## asyncErrorWrapper Utility\n\n### Pattern\n\n```typescript\nexport function asyncErrorWrapper(\n    handler: (req: Request, res: Response, next: NextFunction) => Promise<any>\n) {\n    return async (req: Request, res: Response, next: NextFunction) => {\n        try {\n            await handler(req, res, next);\n        } catch (error) {\n            next(error);\n        }\n    };\n}\n```\n\n### Usage\n\n```typescript\n// Without wrapper - error can be unhandled\nrouter.get('/users', async (req, res) => {\n    const users = await userService.getAll(); // If throws, unhandled!\n    res.json(users);\n});\n\n// With wrapper - errors caught\nrouter.get('/users', asyncErrorWrapper(async (req, res) => {\n    const users = await userService.getAll();\n    res.json(users);\n}));\n```\n\n---\n\n## Error Propagation\n\n### Proper Error Chains\n\n```typescript\n// Ô£à Propagate errors up the stack\nasync function repositoryMethod() {\n    try {\n        return await PrismaService.main.user.findMany();\n    } catch (error) {\n        Sentry.captureException(error, { tags: { layer: 'repository' } });\n        throw error; // Propagate to service\n    }\n}\n\nasync function serviceMethod() {\n    try {\n        return await repositoryMethod();\n    } catch (error) {\n        Sentry.captureException(error, { tags: { layer: 'service' } });\n        throw error; // Propagate to controller\n    }\n}\n\nasync function controllerMethod(req, res) {\n    try {\n        const result = await serviceMethod();\n        res.json(result);\n    } catch (error) {\n        this.handleError(error, res, 'controllerMethod'); // Final handler\n    }\n}\n```\n\n---\n\n## Common Async Pitfalls\n\n### Fire and Forget (Bad)\n\n```typescript\n// ÔØî NEVER: Fire and forget\nasync function processRequest(req, res) {\n    sendEmail(user.email); // Fires async, errors unhandled!\n    res.json({ success: true });\n}\n\n// Ô£à ALWAYS: Await or handle\nasync function processRequest(req, res) {\n    try {\n        await sendEmail(user.email);\n        res.json({ success: true });\n    } catch (error) {\n        Sentry.captureException(error);\n        res.status(500).json({ error: 'Failed to send email' });\n    }\n}\n\n// Ô£à OR: Intentional background task\nasync function processRequest(req, res) {\n    sendEmail(user.email).catch(error => {\n        Sentry.captureException(error);\n    });\n    res.json({ success: true });\n}\n```\n\n### Unhandled Rejections\n\n```typescript\n// Ô£à Global handler for unhandled rejections\nprocess.on('unhandledRejection', (reason, promise) => {\n    Sentry.captureException(reason, {\n        tags: { type: 'unhandled_rejection' }\n    });\n    console.error('Unhandled Rejection:', reason);\n});\n\nprocess.on('uncaughtException', (error) => {\n    Sentry.captureException(error, {\n        tags: { type: 'uncaught_exception' }\n    });\n    console.error('Uncaught Exception:', error);\n    process.exit(1);\n});\n```\n\n---\n\n**Related Files:**\n- [SKILL.md](SKILL.md)\n- [sentry-and-monitoring.md](sentry-and-monitoring.md)\n- [complete-examples.md](complete-examples.md)\n", "sector": "procedural", "salience": 1.0}, {"id": "skill_73", "label": "sub-skills", "group": "industrial", "content": "# Complete Examples - Full Working Code\n\nReal-world examples showing complete implementation patterns.\n\n## Table of Contents\n\n- [Complete Controller Example](#complete-controller-example)\n- [Complete Service with DI](#complete-service-with-di)\n- [Complete Route File](#complete-route-file)\n- [Complete Repository](#complete-repository)\n- [Refactoring Example: Bad to Good](#refactoring-example-bad-to-good)\n- [End-to-End Feature Example](#end-to-end-feature-example)\n\n---\n\n## Complete Controller Example\n\n### UserController (Following All Best Practices)\n\n```typescript\n// controllers/UserController.ts\nimport { Request, Response } from 'express';\nimport { BaseController } from './BaseController';\nimport { UserService } from '../services/userService';\nimport { createUserSchema, updateUserSchema } from '../validators/userSchemas';\nimport { z } from 'zod';\n\nexport class UserController extends BaseController {\n    private userService: UserService;\n\n    constructor() {\n        super();\n        this.userService = new UserService();\n    }\n\n    async getUser(req: Request, res: Response): Promise<void> {\n        try {\n            this.addBreadcrumb('Fetching user', 'user_controller', {\n                userId: req.params.id,\n            });\n\n            const user = await this.withTransaction(\n                'user.get',\n                'db.query',\n                () => this.userService.findById(req.params.id)\n            );\n\n            if (!user) {\n                return this.handleError(\n                    new Error('User not found'),\n                    res,\n                    'getUser',\n                    404\n                );\n            }\n\n            this.handleSuccess(res, user);\n        } catch (error) {\n            this.handleError(error, res, 'getUser');\n        }\n    }\n\n    async listUsers(req: Request, res: Response): Promise<void> {\n        try {\n            const users = await this.userService.getAll();\n            this.handleSuccess(res, users);\n        } catch (error) {\n            this.handleError(error, res, 'listUsers');\n        }\n    }\n\n    async createUser(req: Request, res: Response): Promise<void> {\n        try {\n            // Validate input with Zod\n            const validated = createUserSchema.parse(req.body);\n\n            // Track performance\n            const user = await this.withTransaction(\n                'user.create',\n                'db.mutation',\n                () => this.userService.create(validated)\n            );\n\n            this.handleSuccess(res, user, 'User created successfully', 201);\n        } catch (error) {\n            if (error instanceof z.ZodError) {\n                return this.handleError(error, res, 'createUser', 400);\n            }\n            this.handleError(error, res, 'createUser');\n        }\n    }\n\n    async updateUser(req: Request, res: Response): Promise<void> {\n        try {\n            const validated = updateUserSchema.parse(req.body);\n\n            const user = await this.userService.update(\n                req.params.id,\n                validated\n            );\n\n            this.handleSuccess(res, user, 'User updated');\n        } catch (error) {\n            if (error instanceof z.ZodError) {\n                return this.handleError(error, res, 'updateUser', 400);\n            }\n            this.handleError(error, res, 'updateUser');\n        }\n    }\n\n    async deleteUser(req: Request, res: Response): Promise<void> {\n        try {\n            await this.userService.delete(req.params.id);\n            this.handleSuccess(res, null, 'User deleted', 204);\n        } catch (error) {\n            this.handleError(error, res, 'deleteUser');\n        }\n    }\n}\n```\n\n---\n\n## Complete Service with DI\n\n### UserService\n\n```typescript\n// services/userService.ts\nimport { UserRepository } from '../repositories/UserRepository';\nimport { ConflictError, NotFoundError, ValidationError } from '../types/errors';\nimport type { CreateUserDTO, UpdateUserDTO, User } from '../types/user.types';\n\nexport class UserService {\n    private userRepository: UserRepository;\n\n    constructor(userRepository?: UserRepository) {\n        this.userRepository = userRepository || new UserRepository();\n    }\n\n    async findById(id: string): Promise<User | null> {\n        return await this.userRepository.findById(id);\n    }\n\n    async getAll(): Promise<User[]> {\n        return await this.userRepository.findActive();\n    }\n\n    async create(data: CreateUserDTO): Promise<User> {\n        // Business rule: validate age\n        if (data.age < 18) {\n            throw new ValidationError('User must be 18 or older');\n        }\n\n        // Business rule: check email uniqueness\n        const existing = await this.userRepository.findByEmail(data.email);\n        if (existing) {\n            throw new ConflictError('Email already in use');\n        }\n\n        // Create user with profile\n        return await this.userRepository.create({\n            email: data.email,\n            profile: {\n                create: {\n                    firstName: data.firstName,\n                    lastName: data.lastName,\n                    age: data.age,\n                },\n            },\n        });\n    }\n\n    async update(id: string, data: UpdateUserDTO): Promise<User> {\n        // Check exists\n        const existing = await this.userRepository.findById(id);\n        if (!existing) {\n            throw new NotFoundError('User not found');\n        }\n\n        // Business rule: email uniqueness if changing\n        if (data.email && data.email !== existing.email) {\n            const emailTaken = await this.userRepository.findByEmail(data.email);\n            if (emailTaken) {\n                throw new ConflictError('Email already in use');\n            }\n        }\n\n        return await this.userRepository.update(id, data);\n    }\n\n    async delete(id: string): Promise<void> {\n        const existing = await this.userRepository.findById(id);\n        if (!existing) {\n            throw new NotFoundError('User not found');\n        }\n\n        await this.userRepository.delete(id);\n    }\n}\n```\n\n---\n\n## Complete Route File\n\n### userRoutes.ts\n\n```typescript\n// routes/userRoutes.ts\nimport { Router } from 'express';\nimport { UserController } from '../controllers/UserController';\nimport { SSOMiddlewareClient } from '../middleware/SSOMiddleware';\nimport { auditMiddleware } from '../middleware/auditMiddleware';\n\nconst router = Router();\nconst controller = new UserController();\n\n// GET /users - List all users\nrouter.get('/',\n    SSOMiddlewareClient.verifyLoginStatus,\n    auditMiddleware,\n    async (req, res) => controller.listUsers(req, res)\n);\n\n// GET /users/:id - Get single user\nrouter.get('/:id',\n    SSOMiddlewareClient.verifyLoginStatus,\n    auditMiddleware,\n    async (req, res) => controller.getUser(req, res)\n);\n\n// POST /users - Create user\nrouter.post('/',\n    SSOMiddlewareClient.verifyLoginStatus,\n    auditMiddleware,\n    async (req, res) => controller.createUser(req, res)\n);\n\n// PUT /users/:id - Update user\nrouter.put('/:id',\n    SSOMiddlewareClient.verifyLoginStatus,\n    auditMiddleware,\n    async (req, res) => controller.updateUser(req, res)\n);\n\n// DELETE /users/:id - Delete user\nrouter.delete('/:id',\n    SSOMiddlewareClient.verifyLoginStatus,\n    auditMiddleware,\n    async (req, res) => controller.deleteUser(req, res)\n);\n\nexport default router;\n```\n\n---\n\n## Complete Repository\n\n### UserRepository\n\n```typescript\n// repositories/UserRepository.ts\nimport { PrismaService } from '@project-lifecycle-portal/database';\nimport type { User, Prisma } from '@prisma/client';\n\nexport class UserRepository {\n    async findById(id: string): Promise<User | null> {\n        return PrismaService.main.user.findUnique({\n            where: { id },\n            include: { profile: true },\n        });\n    }\n\n    async findByEmail(email: string): Promise<User | null> {\n        return PrismaService.main.user.findUnique({\n            where: { email },\n            include: { profile: true },\n        });\n    }\n\n    async findActive(): Promise<User[]> {\n        return PrismaService.main.user.findMany({\n            where: { isActive: true },\n            include: { profile: true },\n            orderBy: { createdAt: 'desc' },\n        });\n    }\n\n    async create(data: Prisma.UserCreateInput): Promise<User> {\n        return PrismaService.main.user.create({\n            data,\n            include: { profile: true },\n        });\n    }\n\n    async update(id: string, data: Prisma.UserUpdateInput): Promise<User> {\n        return PrismaService.main.user.update({\n            where: { id },\n            data,\n            include: { profile: true },\n        });\n    }\n\n    async delete(id: string): Promise<User> {\n        // Soft delete\n        return PrismaService.main.user.update({\n            where: { id },\n            data: {\n                isActive: false,\n                deletedAt: new Date(),\n            },\n        });\n    }\n}\n```\n\n---\n\n## Refactoring Example: Bad to Good\n\n### BEFORE: Business Logic in Routes ÔØî\n\n```typescript\n// routes/postRoutes.ts (BAD - 200+ lines)\nrouter.post('/posts', async (req, res) => {\n    try {\n        const username = res.locals.claims.preferred_username;\n        const responses = req.body.responses;\n        const stepInstanceId = req.body.stepInstanceId;\n\n        // ÔØî Permission check in route\n        const userId = await userProfileService.getProfileByEmail(username).then(p => p.id);\n        const canComplete = await permissionService.canCompleteStep(userId, stepInstanceId);\n        if (!canComplete) {\n            return res.status(403).json({ error: 'No permission' });\n        }\n\n        // ÔØî Business logic in route\n        const post = await postRepository.create({\n            title: req.body.title,\n            content: req.body.content,\n            authorId: userId\n        });\n\n        // ÔØî More business logic...\n        if (res.locals.isImpersonating) {\n            impersonationContextStore.storeContext(...);\n        }\n\n        // ... 100+ more lines\n\n        res.json({ success: true, data: result });\n    } catch (e) {\n        handler.handleException(res, e);\n    }\n});\n```\n\n### AFTER: Clean Separation Ô£à\n\n**1. Clean Route:**\n```typescript\n// routes/postRoutes.ts\nimport { PostController } from '../controllers/PostController';\n\nconst router = Router();\nconst controller = new PostController();\n\n// Ô£à CLEAN: 8 lines total!\nrouter.post('/',\n    SSOMiddlewareClient.verifyLoginStatus,\n    auditMiddleware,\n    async (req, res) => controller.createPost(req, res)\n);\n\nexport default router;\n```\n\n**2. Controller:**\n```typescript\n// controllers/PostController.ts\nexport class PostController extends BaseController {\n    private postService: PostService;\n\n    constructor() {\n        super();\n        this.postService = new PostService();\n    }\n\n    async createPost(req: Request, res: Response): Promise<void> {\n        try {\n            const validated = createPostSchema.parse({\n                ...req.body,\n            });\n\n            const result = await this.postService.createPost(\n                validated,\n                res.locals.userId\n            );\n\n            this.handleSuccess(res, result, 'Post created successfully');\n        } catch (error) {\n            this.handleError(error, res, 'createPost');\n        }\n    }\n}\n```\n\n**3.
